## Minsorran

Minsorran was a small theocratic realm on Lake Weng in Amn known as the Lands of Balance, founded during the time of the Shoon Imperium.

Geography
Minsorran was built on the shore of Lake Weng (then called the Lake of Amin) below the Cloud Peaks at an altitude of .

History
The region had long been part of the Calim Empire and its successors, but urban human settlement only arose in the centuries following 100 DR during which Athkatla, Crimmor, and Murann were founded.

Minsorran was founded in the  during the height of the Shoon Imperium by the followers of Selûne, Shar, Lathander, and Mystra. These faithful lived in harmony with each other and respected their collective pantheon as they built a theocracy in Minsor Vale and three other cities, in an uncommon practice that more resembled the religious governments of Unther and Mulhorand than that of western Faerûn.

In the, the emperor Shoon VII gave governorship of the region around the Lake of Amin to Thealnak of Memnon as a reward for creating the Codex Thealnakkar. This political development had little effect on the Lands of Balance.

Discontented Selûnites broke from Minsor Vale in the  and founded Myth Lharast hundreds of miles away, seeking to worship Selûne in a manner that involved fewer Sharrans.

The collapse of the surrounding Shoon Imperium and the rise of competing successor states in 450 DR had little effect on Minsorran.

Minsorran ceased to exist in the  during a harsh midwinter blizzard when the entire population apparently vanished without a trace. The cause was actually the white dragon Icehauptannarthanyx, who laired in Mount Speartop and kept his existence secret. Among those that disappeared were the wife and eldest son of King Esmel Torlath II of Amn, who shortly after died of grief.

All four cities of the realm fell to ruin, though Minsor Vale was eventually resettled with a small village amid the larger ruins. The other three ruins remained uninhabited.

## Mig

Mig was an ogress follower of the Absolute in the Moonrise Towers during the late 15 century DR.

History
In the, Mig was among those who joined the new religions that sprang in the Western Heartlands. The ogress joined after being promised food – meat. She was stationed within the Moonrise Towers – the fortress controlled by the Absolute's warlord – General Ketheric Thorm. Mig was seemingly the only cultist who paid attention to moving sounds all throughout the towers. These sounds came from the colossal spread of the Netherbrain's tentacles deep in the walls of the Moonrise – the true identity of the Absolute. Slithering sounds of the Absolute made Mig hungry.

According to some, Mig may had been slain during the True Soul heroes and Harper attack on the Moonrise Towers that ended Ketheric Thorm's undead existence.

## Mitchifer

Mitchifer was the proprietor and barkeep of the World Serpent Inn, a interplanar tavern and inn that could be accessed from the city of Arabel in Cormyr.

Description
At first glance, Mitchifer appeared like a tall, overweight human with a long white beard and flushed cheeks. If one looked closely at Mitchifer's face, it could be seen that the strands of his beard were tiny white snakes rather than hair. Mitchifer had no irises, and his pupils were shaped like doorways.

It was said, however, that Mitchifer's appearance—as that of his inn—varied at different times and places, sometimes radically.

Personality

Mitchifer was kind to everyone, treating all as friends that he had always known—even if they had treated him poorly in the past. His face seemed stuck in a permanent smile. He had a boisterous, loud laugh, and some—such as Volothamp Geddarm—reported being unable to refrain from laughing in his presence.

Mitchifer was happy to provide basic information about his inn or connect people of common interests, but he refused to talk about more complicated matters.

Abilities
Despite his jovial appearance, Mitchifer had deific powers, which kept violence in his tavern at bay. He sometimes seemed able to control to which plane the inn would connect.

Possessions
Mitchifer seemed to have any drink imaginable available at his bar.

Relationships
Mitchifer had a deal with Shult, the owner of the Wild Goose, an inn in Arabel that had a a dimensional connection to the World Serpent. Mitchifer provided Shult with exotic goods from across the Planes for his patrons.

Mitchifer had a superior known as "the Owner", who was said to be a god or even an overgod, who was a sleeping fragment of the World Serpent.

Rumors & Legends

Many believed that Mitchifer was a refugee from a cosmic faction. Elminster and others believed that Mitchifer was a god who enjoyed interacting with all manner of creatures and chose to become the barkeep of the World Serpent to control access in and out of varying planes. In any case, the Owner's consciousness controlled the demiplane in which the inn had been constructed, and Mitchifer acted as divine servant of that consciousness.

Some claimed that Mitchifer had died multiple times, but he always seemed to return.

In the, Midnight Star wine was brought to the year-end celebration festival in the town of Mossbridges in the Vast. Trendel P. Butterworth, a representative of Sindarian house, entered the wine into a wine-tasting and grading competition. Butterworth's sales pitch claimed that the wine came from a place called "Al'Quidm" and was produced out of grapes gathered only during moonless nights by the city's twenty most beautiful women. ## Ming-hua Dai

Ming-hua Dai was a ghostly sorcerer lieutenant who served Nan Kung Chi in the Crypt of Durkon in Kara-Tur in the 14 century DR.

Possessions
In his life and during the undead existence, Ming-hua Dai was adorned in a purple mage's robe and was armed with simple darts and a quarterstaff.

Abilities
Dai's sorcerous gift allows him to weave many powerful arcane spells, including power word, stun, pierce magic, chain lightning, breach, stoneskin, and many others.

History
Ming-hua Dai died many moons before the late 14 century DR. In life, he was a human spell-slinger and one of six trusted lieutenants who served Nan Kung Chi. He continued to do the same long after death. In the, the crypt faced an illithid incursion led by Goxxa the alhoon. The mind flayers consumed ancient preserved minds of the crypt's dead and sought the Claw of the Black Leopard. At the same time, a vampire thief named Hexxat arrived at the tomb on the behest of her master Larloch, who sought the tomb's magical treasure. A ghostly monk named Ki Chin Sang stood guard of Nan Kung Chi's tomb and demanded anyone seeking entrance to defeat six of Nan Kung Chi's trusted undead lieutenants who all were interred together. When Hexxat and her allies challenged the lieutenants to battle, Ming-hua Dai was joined by Weimin Min, Yi Niu, Borok-Tosst, and Jiang Dai, and, presumably, was defeated.

## Miners' League

The Miners' League was a prominent guild of miners and ore extractors based in the Eileanar enclave during ancient Netheril.

Organization
The guild was owned by the arcanist Gneissenau, with the assistance of several managers. Philo and Lebreton managed the Delver's Consortium that made up the guild's 300 miners, Jackson oversaw the extraction of metals, while Wackernagel and Ochsenbein handled the various arcanists and seers who divined the locations of rich metal deposits around the world.

Activities
The League specialized in the mining and extraction of gold. A great breakthrough came about in  when Gneissenau developed a surprisingly simple process that allowed for completely pure gold to be extracted from ore, using only mercury and portals to the planes of Fire, Vacuum, and Ice. This allowed them to produce pure gold in a consistent and relatively cheap manner.

## Mike Krahulik

Michael Krahulik is an American artist and streamer. His games are the origin of Acquisitions Incorporated and Jim Darkmagic (his player character). He was the cover artist and a writer for the Acquisitions Incorporated sourcebook and adventure.

He is primarily the artist of the webcomic Penny Arcade, written with Jerry Holkins. Together with Holkins, Krahulik created the Penny Arcade Expo beginning in 2004, a series of gaming festivals held in Seattle, Boston, Australia, Philadelphia, and San Antonio. He is also a co-founder of Child's Play, a charity which organizes toy drives for children's hospitals. Along with Holkins, Krahulik was included on the 2010 Time 100 for their work on Penny Arcade.

## Midsummer Festival

The Midsummer Festival was a festival held in the Plain of Horses. It was one of the more commonly observed among the few formal holidays and festivals held in that land.

Activities
Occurring around Kythorn 20,While this is undated and Toril's calendars have different Midsummers, this is presumed to be the one immediately after spring, owing to the animals being born then. This is also the summer solstice, the traditional date for Midsummer on Earth, which is sometimes adopted in the Forgotten Realms. In any case, the Plain of Horses is unlikely to use Faerûn's Calendar of Harptos, but the Kara-Turan calendar is not defined well enough. it was a week-long period of banqueting and celebrating livestock born in the spring.

## Mindi

Mindi was a child fan of the dancer known as Shimmer from the Blushing Mermaid in Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Mindi was a young girl with curly blonde hair.

History
In the, during the Absolute crisis, Shimmer was a regular performer who entertained the customers of the shady Blushing Mermaid tavern in the Lower City of Baldur's Gate. Shimmer was a popular performer known for his loud makeup, glittering outfits, and impressive dancing. He had one devoted fan—the young Mindi, who was in fact too young to frequent the Blushing Mermaid, according to her father. The child passionately begged Shimmer to become his apprentice, believing him to be the best dancer she'd ever seen, and she already was a great dancer, in her own words, able to do two cartwheels in a row. She offered the services of a makeup artist, outfit washer, and a hairstylist in exchange for apprenticeship.

## Michallon

Michallon was a corrupt Netherese arcanist who served as the Chief Constable at Constabulary Branch Two in Eileanar before its destruction in .

Activities
Michallon led the constables of the branch and liaised with Karsus and his assistants. He was aided by his equally unscrupulous Underchiefs Bengel, Ezeddeen, Hacklander, and Bias, who managed the other constables directly. Michallon used his deadly spells and contacts in the criminal world to gain a position of power that straddled law and crime, using his constabulary to quash any criminal elements that threatened his dark allies. His Underchiefs kept loyalty out of a mixture of fear and greed, and together they engendered a sense of awe and respect among the junior constables, who thought that their commanders were truly upstanding and heroic people, Michallon most of all.

## Mistbridge

Mistbridge was the point at which the Long Road crossed the River Erech. It was the only notable crossing of this river, which served as the boundary between Narfell to the west and Rashemen to the east.

Geography
It was located along the River Erech north of the Falls of Erech.While The Forgotten Realms Atlas (p. 7) shows Mistbridge north of the Falls of Erech, Unapproachable East (p. 129) shows the Long Road crossing the river south of the Falls, although Mistbridge is not labeled on this map. It is thus unclear whether Mistbridge is north or south of the Falls.

Trade
Mistbridge was part of the extensive trade route that passed along the Long Road, which connected Rashemen all the way to Damara.

History
Mistbridge was the site of an event that made the Simbul infamous across Faerûn. Having learned that the Red Sword mercenary company had been hired by the Red Wizards of Thay, the Simbul intercepted them at Mistbridge to try to dissuade them from attacking her land of Aglarond. When they not only refused to parlay but dared to attacked her, she flew into a rage and single-handedly annihilated all 2,000 mercenaries with her fiery spells. The story of this slaughter was the single best-known tale about the Simbul's power as of the mid–14 century DR.

## Mike Selinker

Mike Selinker was a game designer and member of the Role-Playing Group Association (RPGA) who designed dozens of tournament modules, including at least one for the Living City program. He marshaled at one or more Gen Cons as his notorious Living City character, the scam artist Discount Marlin. Outside of Dungeons & Dragons, he also worked on the BattleTech Trading Card Game and the Marvel Super Heroes Adventure Game. Mike later acted as a creative director for 3 edition D&D and went on to become the president of Lone Shark Games.

History
Mike got his start as a tournament module author in the RPGA in 1985, having been brought in by director Harold Johnson.

When Mike later acted as one of three authors for the Book of Challenges in 3 edition, he included a subtle reference to his Living City character Discount Merlin in one of the book's puzzles, giving a bugbear dialogue that was verbatim lines he had said in the past.

Between November 2 and 3 of 2013, Mike was one of many participants in a 25-hour marathon that Wizards of the Coast held on the D&D Twitch TV channel as part of the Extra Life charity event, having rotating groups of players run through the adventure module Ghosts of Dragonspear Castle. He played in a group alongside Ben Petrisor, Derek Guder, and Stephen Toulouse.

Works
Core Sourcebooks
 Masters of the Wild (February 2002)
 Book of Challenges (June 2002)
Living City Modules
 The Tarots of the Tarot Hut (August 20, 1992)
Magazine Adventures
 Renegade's Run in Polyhedron #81 (March 3, 1993)
Magazine Articles
 The For-Rest Inn, an article detailing the location of the same name, in Polyhedron #49 (September 1, 1989)
 Power Word: Baffle!, an article on how to design word puzzles, in Dragon #271 (May 2000)
 Logic Missiles, an article on how to design logic puzzles, in Dragon #282 (April 2001)

## Mirela Thesk

Mirela Thesk was an unfortunate priestess of Selûne from Glister who was accused of heresy.

History

Mirela and her sisters Fela and Iwna tended to the shrine at the then-trade moot of Glister until 1180 DR, when they were accused of participating in the New Moon Heresy. The three sisters did not survive the accusations, and it was only in 1194 DR that a formal inquest into the matter was performed by the House of the Moon in Thentia. The House of the Moon came to the conclusion that the charges were false and that only Selûne could judge them, formally absolving them of any misdeeds.

The accounts of this tragedy eventually found their way to Moonshadow Hall, and were discovered in 1373 DR by High Moonmistress Dhauna Myritar as part of her investigations.

## Mist-Walker

The Mist-Walker was the ship of Captain Carreigh Macumail, one of the few human vessels permitted to travel to Evermeet.

History
In 1367 DR, Carreigh abandoned his unsuccessful career as a pirate on the Sea of Fallen Stars and took up a new ship and crew on the Sword Coast as a somewhat more legitimate adventurer. After a few months with the Mist-Walker, he was hired by his friend Aerilaya to transport her to Evermeet on the Retreat. Along the voyage from Corwell, the Mist-Walker aided a swanship being attacked by the infamous pirate Jaq Meadu and rescued the beleaguered elves, took on the priestess Daenalaia and Eilistraeean emissary Karsel'lyn Lylyl-Lytherraias, and finally landed at Leuthilspar.

## Mightywand

A rod of many wands, mightywand, or gonne of many wands was a device capable of triggering a number of wands at the same time.

Description
Usually, a rod of many wands was a mace-like construction with a haft wrapped in leather. The far end was about twice as thick, with three depressions on which wands were to be mounted.

Powers
A rod of many wands allowed the wands mounted on it to be activated as one with a long, complex Draconic invocation, at the cost of draining additional charges from all wands.

History
The War Wizard Belantra in Cormyr used a version that had a full half-dozen wands instead in the, on the night on which Elminster Aumar, the Chosen of Mystra, came to talk to Caladnei. Whatever those wands were, triggering three blasted apart the flagstones from under Elminster's feet, ripped up the ceiling, and generally raised a cloud of dust. One of the wands, whether it was triggered with the three or not, launched a green ray that melted flesh.

Reputation
Elminster later claimed he'd helped Vangerdahast enspell the device in use by Belantra, and it was annoying to see it wasted in a mightywand of the Lantanna fashion, strongly implying the device resembled a Lantanna gonne of many wands.

Notable Owners
 Belantra

## Mirie

Mirie was an Attendant of Grief in the House of Grief temple that hid the Cloister of Sombre Embrace in the city of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Mirie was a wood elven woman with light and freckled skin. She had blonde hair and green eyes. As an attendant and a clerk, she wore simple priestly tunic and carried a quarterstaff.

History
In the, during the Absolute crisis, Mirie served the Mother Superior as one of her Attendant of Grief at the House of Grief. She welcomed guests and those searching for the temple's services in achieving sweet oblivion from grief, pain, melancholy, or treachery. She directed guests to the ritual of Mapping of the Heart that determined the supplicants' true thoughts and intentions.

## Mirrindith

Mirrindith the Last was the Twelfth City of the Sword of ancient Jhaamdath, located in the region of central Faerûn later known as the Vilhon Reach.

History
Mirrindith was established in the  as the final City of the Sword.

Jhaamdath entered into a short period of imperialistic expansion in the, which resulted in the deforestation of the Chondalwood and the slaughter of the Nikerymath elves. The elves responded in the, by casting a High Magic ritual that caused a huge wave from the Inner Sea to wash over the heartlands of Jhaamdath, from the Bay of Jhaamdath to distantly inland Tuoxent. The cities were drowned in the new Vilhon Reach, and Mirrindith became a sunken ruin imbued with stray psionic energy from its lost relics.

The sunken realm of Jhaamdath was suddenly revealed by lowered sea levels following the Spellplague in 1385 DR, becoming the Vilhon Wilds. The situation was then returned to its previous state in 1486 DR by the Great Rain and Second Sundering.

## Misha (vampire spawn)

Misha was a vampire spawn Gur child from the city of Baldur's Gate during the late 15 century DR.

Description
Misha was a human boy with a somber facial expression and a scar on his cheek.

History
In the, Ulma led her monster hunter tribe to the city of Baldur's Gate, where they found themselves targeted by vampires. During the Absolute crisis, when the Gurs camped in the Rivington district of the city, along with a small army of refugees displaced by the conflict, the tribe's children were abducted by Astarion Ancunín under orders from Cazador Szarr.

Misha was the fourth child abducted, and posters with charcoal drawings of his somber face were found in the Lower City, promising a reward for any information on the child's fate. Misha, along with other abducted Gur children, was turned into a vampire spawn to be used in a hellish ritual by Cazador. Subsequently, Astarion Ancunín found himself broken out from under Cazador's control and, along with the Absolute Adversaries, sought to infiltrate his old master's home. The outcome of Astarion's infiltration and the fate of the Gur children remained unconfirmed.

## Mirini Elemys

Mirini Elemys was an elven former slave of Sschindylryn during the mid-1480s DR who escaped to Cormyr.

Description
Mirini had long brown hair, gray eyes, and pale skin. In her escape, she was dressed in ragged bits of clothing with no shoes.

Personality

Mirini was resourceful, driven, and dedicated, and devoted her efforts towards destroying her tormentors.

History

Mirini was taken from her woodland home in a drow slave raid, and spent years as a prisoner of House Avithoul, a drow House famed for its slavery. She was handled by Solul the Slavebreaker, a particularly vicious drow man, and brutalized into submission with physical and magical tortures. Finally, she was controlled with a drow slave collar, which kept a slave's body obedient to the commands of the nearest slavemaster even as their mind rebelled.

During her time there, Mirini earned the (relative) good graces of the House Matron, Balbaste Avithoul, and was given special treatment—that is, kept close to more easily torment on a whim. This allowed Mirini to closely observe Avithoul's compound and their portals which led elsewhere in the Underdark. Around the time of the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, Mirini finally managed to disable her own slave collar through luck and diligence (normally a lethal trick to attempt), and having won control of her own body, she stole a wand of invisibility, leapt through one of the Matron's portals, and fled through the Underdark to freedom.

Mirini eventually made her way to Eveningstar and was interviewed by the Wizard of War Aptem Mews about her escape. The details she provided gave great insights into the inner structure of the drow invasion—particularly the alliance between Houses Avithoul, Szind, and Dun'Robar, and the finer details of Avithoul's own operations. With the support of Cormyr's military, Mirini organized the downfall of House Avithoul by smuggling in agents disguised as slaves. The raid was a great success, and a full-fledged slave revolt broke out, killing many members of House Avithoul and depriving them of their valuable merchandise. This also helped weaken the alliance between the three houses and greatly affected their war against Cormyr.

## Misty Court

Misty Court was a humble and perpetually foggy street in the Tumbledown district of Baldur's Gate's Outer City.

History
In the summer of the, on Kythorn 28, was visited by Tav, an adventuring detective who was investigating a series of grizzly murders. One of the dead bodies was fished out of River Chionthar, wrapped in a tarp taken from one of the workshops on Misty Court. Tav found the open-air stonemason's workshop called Alabaster Stonecrafts. The twins who ran the workshop told the investigator that strange happenings had been plaguing the nearby Cliffside Cemetery, claiming that the gravedigger – Grom Skullsmash, was responsible, or the ghosts.

## Mind over body

Mind over body was a minor psionic devotion of the psychometabolism discipline that allowed a psionicist to overcome their natural need for sustenance and rest for a period of days.

Effects
In exchange for one hour of meditation per day, mind over body enabled a psion to either suppress or mentally satisfy their body's basic physical needs: food, water, and sleep. While the power was active, those affected would not feel hunger, thirst, or exhaustion.

Additionally, a psion could suppress the basic needs of others. This required the psion to meditate another hour for each person they wished to impose the effect upon and for each person to be in physical contact with the psion for the whole duration of the meditation. This physical contact was usually achieved through joining hands together, forming a continuous chain or circle of people.

At the minimum, mind over body could suppress basic needs for a period of five days, though experienced psions were capable of extending the period of effect much further. Upon the power reaching the end of its duration, the psion and anyone else that was under the effects of mind over body would collapse in exhaustion and require a day of rest for every two days the effect was active for, with two days of rest at the least. Alternatively, all those that were under the power's effect could be fully restored to normal by undergoing 24 hours of the psionic devotion complete healing.

Users
Mind over body was exhibited as an innate power by psurlons and pyreens, the latter an ancient and mysterious humanoid race of psionicists native to the desert world of Athas.

Githyanki necromancers often possessed this devotion.

Known/Notable Users
 Ignious Rammousdammous, a tinker gnome and psionic wild talent who used mind over body to operate spelljamming helms for up to seven days without rest, an act that earned him great renown among those who piloted spelljammers.
 Lertis the Seer, a Banite high priest and psionic wild talent, who used mind over body to sustain his body while he spent extended lengths of time traveling across the Realms through astral projection.
 Nar'Lheeth, an illithid psionicist.

## Misker's Warehouse (Heralds' Meet)

Misker's Warehouse was a warehouse on Heralds' Meet in the Lower Port of Skullport in the late 14 century DR.

Structure
The warehouse was a two-story structure in poor condition.

Description
The warehouse was notable for its lack of visible security measures. Despite this, the warehouse remained untouched by thieves or the overly curious. Its security relied entirely on the formidable reputation of its owner, Misker, known as the Pirate Tyrant—a beholder feared throughout Skullport. Although Misker rarely visited his warehouse in person, the fear inspired by his name and his numerous loyal minions effectively protected the property without the need for overt security.

This particular warehouse stored a range of legitimate goods, all part of Misker's complex trading operations. The only protective mechanism within the building was a large pit trap, carefully cultivated by Misker over decades, filled with a layer of deadly green slime. This trap served as a final deterrent to any intruders bold enough to venture inside.

## Misker's Warehouse

Misker's Warehouse was a warehouse in the Lower Port of Skullport in the late 14 century DR.

Structure
The warehouse was a three-story building in fair condition.

Description
Like Misker's other properties, this warehouse appeared entirely unguarded from the outside. However, it was secretly protected by a series of lethal traps and at least one highly convincing illusion, designed to deter intruders and maintain the facade of an ordinary storage facility.

The warehouse held a variety of smuggled goods, carefully concealed within hidden compartments in standard containers such as crates, barrels, and sacks. These containers outwardly contained only mundane trade items, masking the more valuable or illicit contents within. This deceptive setup allowed Misker to carry out his complex smuggling operations while avoiding unwanted attention.

## Misker's Warehouse (Trashtide Alley)

Misker's Warehouse was a warehouse in the Lower Port of Skullport in the late 14 century DR.

Structure
The warehouse was a two-story building in fair condition with an unremarkable exterior. It was the smallest and least imposing of Misker's three warehouses.

Description
The warehouse housed Misker's most precious goods, including stolen gold coins, gems, jewelry, art objects, and, on occasion, captive individuals.

Interior
The first floor appeared entirely empty, save for a stairway leading to the upper level, but it concealed a complex, lethal trap. This trap employed a sophisticated mechanism of weights, counterweights, ropes, pulleys, and dangerously sharp implements. Those unaware of its existence were likely to suffer severe, and potentially fatal, injuries upon entering.

The second floor contained Misker's treasures, secured within locked and heavily trapped iron strongboxes. To further protect these valuables, Misker sometimes employed a basilisk or hired a medusa as a guardian. As a result, he was known to sell realistic stone "statues" to collectors—figures resembling fighters frozen in mid-action, which may indeed be unfortunate trespassers who met their end by those guarding Misker's contraband.

Interior
The interior of the mansion was reportedly filled with rare and strange art that reflected the Misker's alien aesthetics, and its floors were notably devoid of ramps or stairways, rendering much of it inaccessible to those unable to levitate or fly.

## Midler

The Midler family was a farming household that lived near the village of Eveningstar in Cormyr during the 15 century DR, led by the family patriarch Ricnan Midler. They were joined there by the Chandler family, who had married into the Midlers.

History
During the Darkening and the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar in the mid-1480s DR, the Midler farmhouse outside of Eveningstar was overtaken by the cruel priestess of Lolth Vicala Szind. Vicala's forces, including her pet spider Akorstra and Warleader Speitar Dun'Robar, held the entire extended family hostage while interrogating them for clues as to the location of the Thread of the Weave. Their cook Maisby managed to evade detection and hid in the shed behind the house, waiting there for a full night and day before stealthily moving to Eveningstar to get help.

In response to the call for aid, two Purple Dragons including Vicben Arroway moved to investigate, only to be overcome by the drow invaders. A team of adventurers were sent next, who found that Vicben and the whole Midler family had been magically linked to Vicala Szind, allowing her to drain their life force to replenish her own, and forcing the adventurers to handle the situation very carefully. While Vicben was slain, the rest of the Midler family may have been rescued safely without Vicala having the chance to sacrifice them as her shields.

Regardless of the outcome, their servant Maisby moved on to hopefully calmer employment, only to become part of a vampire plot at Sable Manor.

## Mizzen Mast

The Mizzen Mast was a business in the Lower Trade Lanes of Skullport in the late 14 century DR.

Structure
The Mizzen Mast was a three-story building in good condition.

Description
The Mizzen Mast specialized in the construction of a wide range of watercraft, guided by the motto, “No boat too large or small.” Owned and operated by Zulthir "Spyglass" Hestern, a seasoned craftsman with decades of experience, the Mizzen Mast constructed everything from simple rafts to grand galleons. Known for delivering quality results at reasonable prices, Zulthir's work was particularly favored by smugglers, who valued his expertise in crafting hidden compartments within vessels, ideal for concealing illicit cargo.

During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, Mikhael was recovering in the Eveningstar War Hospital when Peron Uthe arrived for treatment. Peron was infected with a strange disease passed from the druid Halsaime, which turned him into a red musk zombie and began spreading to the other patients. Mikhael was among those infected, and eventually transformed into a zombie before being destroyed when the hospital was cleansed. ## Mighty Groznek

Mighty Groznek was a hagspawn warrior who was imprisoned in the jail of Mulsantir in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Groznek was a strong, muscled, brutish man of some girth. He had grey hide, red eyes, long brown hair, and notable tusks. He held spiritual beliefs typical for Rashemen – Groznek venerated spirits he believed to be everywhere – in the rocks, in the grass, in the water of the land.

History
Mighty Groznek was the name the orc gained in battle. Because of his hag parentage, Groznek was considered to be an abomination, unworthy of existing. He existed as an outcast. In the, while in Mulsantir, three people taunted Groznek into a berserker rage. The warrior's sword swiftly ended all three lives, landing the hagspawn in prison. Soon after, the adventurer known as the Knight-Commander, or Kalach-Cha, roused the spirits of Rashemen. Soon after the Knight-Commander's arrival to Mulsantir, an army of spirits, telthors led by the Bear-God Okku, gathered outside and demanded the life of the Knight-Commander. The adventurer approached Tirzah the Old, the witch-warden of Mulsantir's jail was to help find allies, willing or unwilling, who would stand beside the Knight-Commander against angry spirits. Tirzah insisted that hagspawn named Gannayev-of-Dreams, Mighty Groznek, and hin Joeb the Cutpurse were not to be trusted. When the offer of freedom was extended to the orc, Groznek declined despite being a fierce warrior. He refused to raise a weapon against telthors – their memories and influence were great. And Groznek looked forward to achieving peace that the execution would bring.

## Midler farmstead

The Midler farmstead was a farm west of the village of Eveningstar in the realm of Cormyr. It was home to the extended Midler family and their cook Maisby in the late 15 century DR.

Description
The farmhouse was a large two-story building with an attic and cellar, some outlying farm buildings, and surrounding fields.

History
During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, the farmstead was overtaken by drow forces led by the priestess Vicala Szind. She captured the entire family and began interrogating them, hoping that they could provide clues leading to the Thread of the Weave that Lolth sought. Unfortunately for Vicala, not only did the Midlers know nothing about it, but Maisby escaped her notice and hid in the farm shed before fleeing to Eveningstar to get help. She was taken care of by the villager Helena Falagon while Vicben Arroway and another Purple Dragon went to secure the farmstead, only to be caught and sacrificed by Vicala Szind. A second team was sent in, this time of adventurers, who had to face not just Vicala's forces, but her ability to ritually sacrifice her prisoners to preserve herself. Though difficult, it was possible that they managed to free the captive Midlers by stealth before openly attacking the priestess.

Whatever the outcome, Maisby went on to work as a servant at Sable Manor, hoping for a calmer employment.

## Michelle Vuckovich

Michelle Vuckovich, once called Jill-of-all-Trades, is a former assistant editor, and later, editor of the Dragon and Dungeon magazines, as well as several Dungeons & Dragons products, including The North: Guide to the Savage Frontier and Demihumans of the Realms.

Biography
Michelle resides in San Francisco, CA and has over twenty years of experience working on such entertainment formats as magazines, books, tabletop RPG sourcebooks, video games, multimedia museum tours. Among well known companies she's worked with are TSR, Wizards of the Coast, Nickelodeon, Zynga, Sega, Lucasfilm, Google.

Michelle joined TSR in 1995. She worked for TSR's Periodicals Department as an editorial assistant on Dragon Magazine issues 215 through 223. As of issue 218, Michelle started being credited for writing Dragon's Rumblings section dedicated to industry news, internal staff updates, as well as other updates. And she headlined Women on the Verge editorial in issue 216 dedicated to women in gaming. She also worked as an editorial assistant for Dungeon Magazine starting with issue 52 and through 56. As of Dragon Magazine 224, and Dungeon 56, Michelle received credits as an associate editor. She was promoted to the editor of Dungeon starting with issue 61 in 1996 and continued her indispensable work until Dragon issue 240 in 1997, in which editor Dave Gross announced Michelle's discussion to leave TSR, who was planning to resume her education – Master's degree. In an editorial message in Dungeon 65, Christopher Perkins jokingly claimed that he has banished Michelle from gaming industry for criticizing the monstrous length of his published adventures. During her time with TSR, Michelle also lended her editor's skills to Dragonlance Classics: 15 Anniversary Edition, The Shattered Circle, as well as two Forgotten Realms sourcebooks.

Since leaving TSR, Michelle worked as an editor for Lucasfilm, notably editing the Power of the Jedi Sourcebook, the Ultimate Adversaries sourcebook, and The Official Star Wars Fact File issues 81 through 93. She departed Lucasfilm in 2003.

She currently works as a senior games product specialist for Google in San Francisco.

Apart from working in gaming, tabletop, and editing, Michelle's name popped up in San Francisco Chronicle news in 2007 when it was reported that her car received 29 tickets from the city's meter maids after it was stolen. After receiving the tickets through mail, Michelle contacted the police only to be faced with disinterest and not willingness to take action. She set out to track the car herself through the meter maid tickets, finding just two blocks away from a police station.

## Mind flayer clairvoyant

Clairvoyants were mind flayers who forsook the Prime Material and their race's expected behavior of serving elder brains, instead turning to the Far Realm and the myriad of eldritch entities that whispered within its depths.

Abilities
Like illithid psions, clairvoyants were capable of remarkable feats of psionic power, akin to clairvoyance, detect magic, dispel magic, and mage hand. In addition to theor innate abilities, clairvoyants also displayed extraordinary powers from having peered into thestarless depths of the Far Realm. Typically they could open rifts into that plane, unleashing tentacles that distorted the minds of their foes, and warp reality to teleport attackers  away. They also hovered above the ground and had slightly greater perception than standard illithids, possessing both darkvision and truesight, though could not perceive the latter as far as their normal senses.

History
Around the, a group of mind flayers laired beneath the Sword Coast North that worshiped a godlet in the Far Realm by the name of Ilvaash and were determined to reestablish the Illithid Empire on the Prime Material plane. Having discovered fragments of a black obelisk near the village of Phandalin, they planned to reconstruct it and infuse the obelisk with Ilvaash's energy to transform the citizenry of Phandalin into illithids loyal to Ilvaash, turning their village the seat of a new illithid empire. Among their number were a trio of clairvoyants – Chishinix, Hashutu, and Voalsh – who as their plan was nearing completion entered Ilvaash's Endless Void and traveled to a place therein called the Anima to carry out the ritual.

The Michell Academy was destroyed many years before the  after antagonizing the local temple dedicated to Loviatar, the Patroness of Torturers. The temple's mistresses led an hour-long battle that ended the Academy. Jamal Briars, the professor of alchemy, received a torture that was a pun on his name. The temple's elementals dragged Jamal across thorny briers until he perished from blood loss.

## Mist wolf

Mist wolves were a somewhat larger cousin of ordinary wolves, with some innate magical abilities and a lawful good nature.

Description
They looked very much like normal wolves expect for their slightly larger stature, often growing to  at their shoulders and having white tips on their hackles of otherwise grey fur. They were usually slim, muscular wolves, with fearsome-looking teeth, and black eyes that lacked the red tinge that ordinary wild wolves had.

Abilities
Their innate magical abilities allowed them to exhale a cloud of thick mist that would block all vision between them and a foe twice a day. This mist filled a volume of  on a side, similar to the wall of fog spell, and lasted for half a minute, allowing the wolf to flee the situation.

All mist wolves had an innate ability to detect evil. This power was constant and worked without conscious volition.

Behavior
They had their own language that consisted of yips, barks, and growls normally understood only by their own kind. They were far more intelligent than normal wolves and understood the Common tongue, but for anatomical reasons were unable to speak it.

They were implacable enemies of evil and defenders of goodness and law, even giving up their own lives to protect humans and demihumans of good alignment. Only humans or demihumans who had acted in a flagrantly evil manner were at risk of being attacked by a mist wolf pack.

Combat
Mist wolves attacked in packs like their common cousins, using their weight of numbers to drag down opponents and raking them with their savage teeth. As they were more intelligent than other wolves, they were more likely to abandon an obviously losing battle, using their mist breath to flee for safety.

Ecology
Mist wolf society was based around the pack, consisting of up to 20 adult wolves, with a 50/50 split between males and females. Ordinarily there were about half as many cubs as females in the pack. Mist wolves were monogamous and would mate for life, with both parents sharing the responsibility of caring for their cubs. At 6 months they gained their mist breath ability and adulthood was reached at 12 months of age.

The strongest of the pack, either male or female, became the leader and they would have to constantly defend their position from would-be challengers in non-lethal combat, losing the role of leader should they lose.

They most commonly inhabited forests with evil reputations as that was where they could do the most good. This of course did not help their reputation at all and added to the unfortunate infighting that they so often experienced with those they sought to help.

Territories claimed by mist wolves were usually rich with game as they were intelligent enough to control their hunting with care, making sure never to over-hunt an area.

Relationships
If their pack was ever destroyed, mist wolves would sometimes take on a supporting role to good rangers, elves, and other people who battled evil in the wilderness.

While mist wolves attempted to protect travelers from evil creatures that might wish them harm, it was common for many people to feel fear and hatred of wolves. As such, it was not uncommon for those whom mist wolves tried to aid to turn their weapons on a mist wolf pack, which would've appeared around the same time as evil foes, and battle their would-be allies. Hence, they were sometimes slain by the very people they tried to protect.

History
The halfling deity Urogalan were served by several packs of mist wolves living in Eronia, the second layer of Elysium.

## Minecraft Dungeons & Dragons: A New Quest

Minecraft Dungeons & Dragons: A New Quest is a standalone DLC adventure for a non-canonical Minecraft Dungeons & Dragons released February 20, 2025 for all gaming platforms.Minecraft Dungeons & Dragons and A New Quest feature an out-of-universe framing narrative that makes the events of both games the player character's session of Dungeons & Dragons, making them non-canonical.

Plot
{{quote|In A New Quest, Katra and Vaer are striving to reunite Vaer’s soul with his body that was stolen by dark forces. During their journey, they reach Candlekeep, where Thesserai the Sage asks Katra and Vaer to aid him in locating his “Deck of Many Things”, hoping it’ll lead them closer to finding Vaer’s lost body.The deck is a mighty set of cards with the power to either stir conflict or prevent it. The deck stems from chaos magic (sounds super safe tbh), and as it now has gone missing, it needs to be returned before it ends up in the wrong hands (or claws).
Katra and Vaer accept the call to adventure, and thus, their perilous journey (and yours!) begins, meeting yetis, and dwarven hunters, recovering treasures, helping one-legged pirates, and discovering widespread conspiracies.}}

## Mithtyn

Mithtyn was the herald of Uthgrael Aumar in the early 3 century DR.

Description
By 240 DR, his voice had grown dry from disuse.

Relationships
In King Uthgrael Aumar's court, he had been the herald.

History
At some point during King Uthgrael's reign, Mithtyn, then the herald, had daced with the individual who would later take the identity of Myrjala Talithyn, though she bore a different look at the time.

After Prince Elminster Aumar's forces retook Athalgard in, Mithtyn was called to court as one of those who had been in Uthgrael's court, per the request of the sorceress Myrjala Talithyn. There, she revealed to him they'd danced before, a long time ago. He was taken aback. During the subsequent dance as a new king was crowned, Mithtyn tried to stay out of the dancing, but the sorceress would have none of that, and with the help of onlookers, made him join the dance.

Mithtyn later waved off Elminster Aumar and Myrjala as they rode away together, though he was uneasy about a prophecy by Alaundo that said the Aumar line would outlive the Stag Throne. Myrjala assured him, so long as Elminster lived, the line would endure. Anxious to tell someone about Myrjala, he found himself approached by the Knight of Athalantar Anauviir, and took him to the kitchens, where he meant to tell him the history as he knew it.

## Mindarion

Lord Mindarion was a leader among the Knights of Synnoria on the island of Gwynneth in the Moonshae Isles, although he abandoned them during the late 15 century DR. He was a key participant in a Synnorian plot to overthrow the ruling leShay of Sarifal.

Description
He was a very old Llewyrr high elf, with high and effeminate cheek bones, bright eyes, and a long nose. His skin and hair were pale, and following a great injury and massive expenditure of his magical power, his skin turned ashen and his hair was streaked with gray.

Personality

Mindarion had an easy demeanor, even when he was not smiling. He drank and savored water as if it were wine.

He was an optimist who was frequently disillusioned with the malaise that his fellow elves developed over the course of their long lifespans. He was willing to overlook ancient enmities for the benefit of a brighter future, but regretted that few of his kin shared this willingness.

Abilities
Mindarion was a potent spellcaster, able to shield himself and his allies from spells and to attack with explosive, elemental power sufficient to level whole sections of forest. However, given his advanced age, he risked exhausting himself if he called upon too much power, potentially leaving him nearly comatose.

Relationships
Mindarion plotted the downfall of High Lady Ordalf of Sarifal, and kept tabs on her forces and her schemes.

He was held in high esteem among the Knights of Synnoria prior to his departure from Synnoria. He regarded one of his comrades, Lord Askepel, to be a particularly egregious example of how the long years of Llewyrr's lifespan could strip someone of their emotion and their love of life. By contrast, he appreciated the urgency of humans and other shorter-lived races, and as such he was known to work alongside Captain Rurik of the Winterglen Claw. He also sought cordial relations with the princess Marabaldia and lord Ughoth of the fomorian tribes who dwelt beneath Sarifal.

He had a daughter named Altaira. His niece was the half-leShay princess of Sarifal, Lady Amaranth.

History
Mindarion had committed his life to overthrowing the sovereigns of Sarifal. He and his fellow conspirators operated out of an abandoned fortress known as Harrowfast that sat on the border of Synnoria. His brother, also a Knight of Synnoria, was killed by Prince Araithe of the leShay following the birth of Amaranth in the late 1450s DR, whose mother had been the leShay princess Callia. Amaranth would subsequently disappear after a botched plot to smuggle her out of Sarifal and away from the machinations of the leShays, and when she was found again in the late 1470s DR, Mindarion made it his mission to make her the new queen of Sarifal. He believed that the only way to motivate his kin to action against their tyrant rulers was to also promise one of their own on the throne. At the same time, he was attempting to broker an alliance with Lord Ughoth and his nine fomorian tribes, without whom the Knights of Synnoria simply would not have the numbers to fight the leShay.

Upon hearing rumors that High Lady Ordalf had learned of Amaranth's location from a prisoner, Mindarion and a handful of his knights joined Captain Rurik and his men to investigate. They tracked Prince Ariathe to the ruins of Caer Corwell as he also investigated the same rumors, and successfully rescued the prisoner, Poke, from Araithe's drow, alongside a fomorian princess named Marabaldia and a gnome named Suka. On their return journey to Harrowfast, Mindarion learned of Amaranth's activities from Poke before she was slain by a drow darkwalker. Defeating the darkwalker took all of Mindarion's strength, and by the time they reached Harrowfast, he was fading quickly and was unable to continue his role in negotiating an alliance between the Knights of Synnoria, the humans of the Winterglen Claw, and the fomorians.

With Mindarion falling into slumber, he was tended to by his daughter Altaira, who believed he was likely to "turn inward" for several years as he recovered. With him unable to participate, negotiations were led by Lord Askepel, who was unable or unwilling to acknowledge the grievances of the humans and fomorians nor the extent to which his Knights required their aid. When negotiations broke down and violence broke out, the humans fled Harrowfast and the elves attacked the fomorians. Mindarion was forcibly roused from his slumber by Suka, and he made a snap decision to take the side of the fomorians by helping them to escape to the Underdark. Giving up on the stubbornness of his people, he resolved to leave them behind, although he invited Altaira to join him in his coming odyssey.

## Mint

Mint was a plant commonly used as a form of seasoning in the Realms, primarily classified as a type of herb. It was noted for its aromatic qualities, sweet flavor, and cool aftertaste.

Availability
 Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold mint under its "Aurora's Larder" section at a cost of 3 copper pieces per, as well as candied mint for 7 silver pieces per,
 In the Dalelands, mint could be found in Archendale, Daggerdale, Deepingdale, and Mistledale.
 Traveling rations in the land of Calimshan often included mint leaves for making tea.
 The Inn of the Weary Pilgrim had a garden of mint.
 In the Sword Coast North, mint sprigs and leaves were sold in the markets of Waterdeep. and in Brant's dry goods in the South Ward.
 In south Faerûn, the port city of Khaerbaal had a public garden where herbs like mint grew.
 In the Vilhon Reach, mint could be purchased in the city of Hlondeth.
 Mint could be found growing wild in the forest of Cormanthor, the Forest of Tethir, in High Forest, and across the Sword Coast North.
 In Interior Zakhara, mint was cultivated in the herb garden of the fortress Sarahin. They was also available in parts of north Zakhara, being enjoyed by Caliph Harin al-Hayif of Liham.

Varieties
 Catmint, a member of the mint family that grew wild in Cormanthor.
 Pale mint, a member of the mint family that was endemic to the scorched sands of the Calim Desert.
 Pennyroyal
 Peppermint
 Spearmint
 Ticklemint, a member of the mint family endemic to the wilds of Icewind Dale.
 Wintermint

Usages
Food & Drink
 Greentart, a gnomish ale served at Le PoTeete Café in Ravens Bluff.
 Mint ale, a Sembian gnomish ale.
 Mint was sometimes candied, much in the same way as one candied fruits.
 Mint jelly.
 Mint leaves were commonly brewed in water to make mint tea in both Faerûn and Zakhara.
 Mint water, water that's been laced with mint to give it a refreshing taste. Whenever water was flavored with mint it was referred to as having been "minted".
 Mint could be used in the making of a variety of wines, including mint wine, the fern-and-mint wine produced by the druids of Kirinwood, and a type of mushroom wine produced by Klarinthra Larntrout.
 Moonslake was made by boiling water with crushed wild mint, then straining that water to remove any leaf residue, before using that water to cut apple cider.
 In some places, kaeth was mixed with mint leaves sprinkled over its surface and prepared by stewing the beans in a pot over a fire or a hearth.
 Runsun, a variety of common trail food—a pie made with meat cooked in spinach-and-mint or spinach-and-hot-pepper simmer sauce.
 One of the listed libations in Volo's Guide to Spirits and Spectres called for a garnish of mint.

Magic
 Mint could be used in the brewing of a variety of different potions, including potions of accuracy, defense, and force.
 Duthlah'hass oil, a type of osssra, was made in part with mint.
Medicinal
 In the Moonsea region, healers from Phlan used juniper and mint as components in their healing ointments. These salves possessed strong but pleasant scent of both. Others from that region used a combination of mint and raspberries to create a medicinal herbal tea.
 Tea could also be made with mint for the purposes of soothing an upset stomach or inducing drowsiness.
 The variant of speak with dead known by Tessaril Winter used sprigs of mint as a material component.
Miscellaneous
 The aromatic quality of mint lent itself to use in tubs of water to make herbal baths. In the port city of Uthmere, this was especially the case when washing hair.
 Some mrathaera was flavored or scented like mint, from its use in the herbal mixtures boiled to make it. This variety was mostly used among lovers desiring to please their partners.
 Could be used to make a green dye.
 Could be combined with other herbs as part of incense.

Notable Users
Businesses
 The Hungry Man, a restaurant in Arabel, typically served mint alongside a platter of boiled carrots and other greens.'
 Moon Mountain Brewery
 The Moonwyvern Inn in Luruar treated the buckets of water that it kept on standby, in case of fires, with mint so as to keep away stagnant odors.
 The Old Wheel, a club in Crimmor, offered a dressing of vegetable juice with mint for its greensplatters.
 One of the peculiar cloaks served at the Owlroost Head Inn in Purkul was a dish of squid-stuffed yams, whose squids were boiled in lime juice laced with lemon slices and mint leaf garnish.
 The Ship's Prow, an inn in Waterdeep, made its own mint water by lacing water brought over from wells near Amphail with crushed mint.
 Stargath House in Purskul was known for it's "Welcome Soup", a creamy stew of smoked bacon, black beans, and chives garnished with mint.
Creatures
 Asperii prized mint leaves.
 Feystags had a fondness for wild mint.
Individuals
 Dawan Pax, a legendary druid active in the North during the 15 century DR, made an invigorating tonic from mint, peaches, and ginger
 The goddess Lurue was widely believed to have a special love for mint.

History
In the, the Downunda Patisserie in Ravens Bluff offered mint & green apple cookies as one of its Summer Specials. The bakery charged 5 cp for a singular cookie and 3 sp for a dozen.

Trivia
 Some of the more expensive healing draughts were said to have a taste profile of clover, honey, mint, and zzar.
 The scent of this herb was one of many that saurials expressed through their glands to communicate emotions. The scent of mint expressed an emotion of remorse.

## Mirror of madness

A mirror of madness was a phenomenon that acted as a viewing portal into the Abyss, functioning as a window; those who gazed upon it risked madness. The mirror was always positioned on a wall.

The mirror of madness was created when Abyssal cults corrupted a location for several years. The mirror recognized the cultists and their allies as allies and any other creatures as enemies, and acted as a trap for the latter. Those attacked could experience effects from immobilization to domination of their will. If the mirror was physically attacked, it would lash out at the surrounding creatures.

Creatures with rogueish, arcane, or divine inclinations had the greatest chances of resisting the mirror's effects and destroying it simply by standing in its proximity. Once destroyed, the mirror's effects ceased.

## Milon Tillerturn

Description
Milon was a handsome human man with a dusky skin tone, dark brown eyes, curly black hair, and a set of bushy muttonchops. Milon wore an expensive ensemble, tan in color, decorated with a teal trim.

History
In the, shortly before the armies of the Cult of the Absolute attacked the city of Baldur's Gate, Lord Milon Tillerturn was a Parliamentarian of the Gate and voted in support of crowning lowborn Lord Enver Gortash as the city's Archduke. Milon was willing to move with the times and throw in his support for a commoner, despite it being an unprecedented occasion. Lord Tillerturn was present for Gortash's coronation that took place in the audience hall of Wyrm's Rock. Following the coronation, Milon Tillerturn conversed with Lady Silifrey Sashenstar near a table of smallbites and libations.

## Mir (kingdom)

The First Kingdom of Mir (called Monrativi Teshy Mir in Alzhedo), or just Mir, was an ancient kingdom in west Faerûn that served as the foundation for the Calimshan Empire, Shoon Imperium, and Calimshan. The formation of Mir was considered the start of the First Age of Calimshan.

Geography
The kingdom was centered on the former dwarven city of Iltakar, which eventually became the site of Shoonach. At its furthest extents the realm stretched west to the Trackless Sea along the River Agis, and as far east to Saelmur and the Lake of Steam.

History
Following the Era of Skyfire and the disappearance of Memnon and Calim around, their former domains fell to destruction and dissolution. The last vestiges of genie rule were eventually destroyed by -6060 DR thanks to the collaboration of Coram the Warrior and the dwarves of Shanatar, creating the new realm of Coramshan atop the ruins of the Calim Empire and Memnonnar. Taking advantage of the lands left behind by the genies' wars, the dwarves founded the realm of High Shanatar around, building settlements across what was later western and southern Tethyr. At the same time, the western Darthiir Wood and northern Marching Mountains gradually became inhabited by exiles and refugees from Coramshan, who wandered and became nomadic families and clans in the region. The pleasant relations between Coramshan and Shanatar ended at, when the dwarves killed tomb robbers on Mount Kellarak that included the eldest heir to the bakkal of Coramshan, starting what would become known as the Shanatar Wars.

The kingdom originated with Murabir Mir, warlord and military leader of Coramshan. Around  he marched with an army from Keltar, traveled around the Calim Desert and the Marching Mountains, and killed or press-ganged the nomadic peoples he encountered. Crossing the River Memnon on rafts, they fell upon the dwarves of High Shanatar while they fought the giants of Nedeheim and conquered their city of Iltakar. By, Murabir Mir had conquered the area around Iltakar up to the Wurlur (the dwarven defense made further northern conquest unappetizing) and west of the Darthiir Wood, founding the Kingdom of Mir as a separate but closely allied entity to Coramshan. Over the next century the Mirrans skirmished with the dwarves of Shanatar and gradually annihilated the local Darthiir Wood elves, forcing them to flee to Nikerymath and the Forest of Tethir.

Despite the constant fighting the realm never lost ground, and Murabir Mir embarked on another ambitious campaign of conquest to the east circa . Thanks to a strong alliance with Coramshan that defended their homeland, Mir was able to devote its full forces to the campaign. By, the kingdom had conquered all of the lands south of the Wurlur in a generations-long invasion of High Shanatar, taking the dwarven lands around the Omlarandin Mountains and extending the frontier east. This territory of Mir remained an uninhabited wilderness for a long time, though it was recorded as the land of Iltkazar. The armies of Mir then approached the Lake of Steam, founding Saradush, Mintar, and Saelmur as military camps as they went. This brought them into conflict with the nascent empire of Jhaamdath as they expanded from the Vilhon Reach and colonized the Lake, finally leading to open war in . The first battle of the Mir–Jhaamdath War was on the plains north of Ankhport, resulting in a painful defeat for Mir. Sensing weakness, Jhaamdath pursued the war through to, grinding the armies of Mir down and pushing them back to Fort Saradush.

With the ascendant army of Jhaamdath on their own border, Coramshan actively joined the war after "the negotiations which deemed unification a necessary gambit to ensure the survival of either realm" (possibly involving the Murabir and his three generals being murdered), leaving Bakkal Ukhar IV of Coramshan also serving as Murabir of Mir. After a year of preparation, the combined forces of Mir and Coramshan counterattacked, using Coramshan's priests and wizards to great effect. The Jhaamdathan forces were shocked by the turnaround and in only two months they were forced back to Saelmur, and after another year they signed a truce. The peace of  led to the mutual dropping of any claims on the Lake of Steam, satisfying both sides.

Having won an incredible victory, Ukhar IV returned home amid the celebration of his people. He announced that he would rule both Mir and Coramshan, and named the new realm Calimshan to evoke the power and terror of the genie from ages past. While Coramshan and Mir continued to exist as political entities, they were suborned to the rule of the Calimshan Empire, and the capital moved to Calimport. By the Third Age of Calimshan  Mir was one of the core kingdoms of the empire, alongside Coramshan, Tethyr, and Iltkazar (the early Mir territories around the Omlarandin Mountains were granted to Iltkazar), each ruled by the pasha in Calimport while more distant lands such as the Calishar Emirates were governed locally.

The Fourth Age of Calimshan ( to ) saw a period of internal focus as Tethyr was colonized and Mir became a center of urban development. Iltakar became the second largest city in the Empire and grew a web of satellite towns and fortresses about it to feed and defend it, one of which was Ithmong. Although Mir survived the subsequent drow attacks, rebellions of Iltkazar and Tethyr, and Empire Plague, it did not long remain part of Calimshan. In thanks for Tethyr's participation in the Eye Tyrant Wars, the Pasha of Calimshan granted King Silvam Ithal the lands of Mir wholesale in, also titling him the Raj of Ankaram (this also disarmed the disloyal Raj of Mir who was planning rebellion).

The ancient lands of Mir once again took primacy in the, when Amahl Shoon II constructed the sprawling urban complexes of Shoonach on the site of Iltakar and made it the capital of the Shoon Imperium. Following the fall of the Imperium, Mir remained broadly associated with southwestern Tethyr and its Duchy Ankaram. The area directly around Shoonach became a desolate realm of ruins called Monrativi Teshy Mir, the subject of nostalgic longings and occasional fighting for revanchism-addled Calishites for centuries to come.

## Minsk

Minsk was a werebat from the Demiplane of Dread that became trapped in the Vast during the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
Minsk may have looked like a young boy, or a young man, with a squeaky voice either way.

Personality
His strategy to get fed was to get in the middle of travelers' groups, isolate them, and then suck their blood when they let their guard down.

Abilities
Being a werebat, he had all normal werebat abilities.

Possessions
He had a crucible of melting, and a globe of purification; Minsk had since taken to melting silver weapons used against him in the crucible, making some interesting silver disks from them.

Relationships
He may have had a wife called Ursala, and he may also have had up to four kids: two sons called Marcov or Rensil, and two daughters, called Olga or Romona. That much was true.

History
Minsk had been drawn from the Demiplane of Dread after Romanisk Vlaensk, a vampire, summoned bats; he became trapped in the Prime Material plane, landing in an area near Stag Falls, unable to find his home again. He may have had the rest of his family with him at the moment, including his wife and up to four children, all of them were actually werebats like him.

About a month after Romanisk Vlaensk's release, Minsk had taken over a new cave, where he kept new treasure, perhaps up to a couple hundred bats, and up to ten giant bats. He tried to feed on a group of adventurers at that time; it is unknown whether he survived the ensuing confrontation, and if he did, whether they pursued to his lair, where they may have slain him.

## Mir (warlord)

Murabir Mir was a Coramshite warlord who created and ruled the First Kingdom of Mir, contributing to the rise of the Calimshan Empire and the decline of the dwarves of Shanatar.

History
Though Coramshan and Shanatar had started as allies, Ambril's Bane led to the Shanatar Wars between the humans of the south and the dwarves of High Shanatar. In  Murabir Mir led a Coramshite warband from Keltar north between the Calim Desert and Marching Mountains, killing and forcibly recruiting the disparate tribes and families that wandered there. They crossed the River Memnon on rafts, and fell upon the High Shanatar city of Iltakar by surprise, conquering it and seizing it from the dwarves. For the next few decades, Mir continued conquering about Iltakar, taking advantage of the Giant Wars to seize more lands from the dwarves south of the Wurlur, and in  he officially founded the Kingdom of Mir as a separate but closely allied realm to Coramshan. After a period of consolidation, the aged Mir launched a new campaign in, seeking to conquer to the east at the Omlarandin Mountains while Coramshan's army protected the Kingdom of Mir. Though he did not live to see it, the Mir territories eventually extended to the Lake of Steam and met the Empire of Jhaamdath, causing the Mir–Jhaamdath War in  and eventually the creation of the Calimshan Empire in .

## Miles Broadleaf

Sir Miles Broadleaf was a knight of Silverymoon who retired to the island of Snowdown in the Moonshae Isles during the first half of the 14 century DR.

Personality
Sir Miles's passion was hunting. He enjoyed the rugged landscape of Snowdown, but he also valued comfort.

History
Originally from the North, Miles was knighted by Alustriel Silverhand of Silverymoon and retired soon after. He relocated to the Moonshae Isles where he constructed a hunting lodge for himself and his guests known as Broadleaf Lodge. He filled the lodge with comfortable furniture and adorned the great hall with his proudest hunting trophy: a rug made from the skin of a dire bear.

Sir Miles died without an heir in the .

## Mileirigath

Mileirigath was a sorcerer of early Netheril, and the one to turn Palarandusk into the form the gold dragon retained into the 15 century DR.

Possessions
Mileirigath owned much magic, mostly in the form of spellbooks.

Activities
The Netherese mage conducted many experiments, especially on Palarandusk.

Relationships
In life, Mileirigath owned Palarandusk, and had numerous apprentices.

History
Mileirigath was a sorcerer of early Netheril. In life, he and many apprentices experimented on Palarandusk; after his death, the dragon in turn looted his wealth and magic. These experiments were responsible for much of Palarandusk's longevity.

## Mielikki's Grove

Mielikki's Grove was a woodland holy site in the King's Forest of Cormyr, dedicated to the goddess of nature and forests Mielikki.

Description

The grove was a picturesque stretch of woodlands, and included a few artificial shrines. The hag Granny Gertrude lived nearby in her hut.

History

Not long after the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar in the late 15 century DR, the Red Wizard Dhovras invaded the grove. He slew a unicorn upon the stones of Mielikki's shrine, then took its remains to be reanimated as a skeletal beast loyal to him. He also began animating the ancient remains of everyone who had perished within the forest, creating a small but growing army of undead. Mielikki felt this outrageous profanity in her very essence and raised up a number of blights to destroy the wizard, but he proved stronger than them. The hag Granny Gertrude was likewise powerless to stop him, as to fight him meant fighting the unicorn, and she had sworn not to harm any creatures of Mielikki's forest. The situation was ultimately resolved when the Evenor forester Jarra Morand noticed the rampaging blights and sent some folks to investigate, upon which they discovered the horrid situation unfolding.

The local heroes found the hag and were willing to believe she was innocent, and then found the shrine soaked in unicorn blood, where Mielikki herself spoke to encourage them onwards. After perhaps cleaning up the bloody scene, they proceeded on and found the Red Wizard working on his unicorn project. They managed to destroy the unfortunate creature and put it to rest, which gave Granny Gertrude the opportunity to strike at Dhovras next. He was captured alive by her magics and taken away to an unknown but undoubtedly nasty fate.

## Mist caves

The mist cavesThe words are uncapitalized whenever outside of section headers. were a collection of caves in the Ice Spires in northwest Faerûn that served as the residence of Goboka, an ogre chieftain, and his forces in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
These caverns varied in size, but were uniformly flooded with a thick, mildly corrosive gas and the stench of death and ogre accoutrements, such as molded clothes, half-eaten animals, and rotting, or rotted, meat.

Geography
There were about 27 caves in all.

Defenses
The mist in the caves and the pervasive stench allowed its nearly 100 ogre defenders to operate in often successful stealth, surprising any attackers.

History
At some point in the 14 century DR, the ogre chieftain Goboka took these caverns as the seat of his government.

## Mikrom Sum

Mikrom Sum was a Purple Dragon of Cormyr, who served in Eveningstar during the invasion from Sschindylryn in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Mikrom was a large and powerful human, with his hair shaved on the sides. He wore a suit of chain mail with a white surcoat.

History
Mikrom oversaw the supply of equipment to Cormyr's forces and their allies, as well as handling repairs and upgrades.
